=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There are N types of products, each having 10^{100} units in stock.
You can buy any non-negative number of units of each product. To buy k units of the i-th product, it costs k^2 P_i yen.
If your total purchase cost is at most M yen, what is the maximum number of units you can buy in total?

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N M
P_1 \ldots P_N

Output

Print the answer.

Constraints


- 1 \leq N \leq 2 \times 10^{5}
- 1 \leq M \leq 10^{18}
- 1 \leq P_i \leq 2 \times 10^{9}
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

3 9
4 1 9

Sample Output 1

3

If you buy one unit of the 1st product and two units of the 2nd product, the total purchase cost is 1^2 \times 4 + 2^2 \times 1 = 8. It is impossible to buy four or more units in total with a total cost of at most 9 yen, so the answer is 3.

Sample Input 2

10 1000
2 15 6 5 12 1 7 9 17 2

Sample Output 2

53

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Let's write code:

import sys

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N, M = map(int, input().split())
    P = list(map(int, input().split()))
    
    def ok(x):
        s = 0
        for p in P:
            c = (x + p) // (p << 1)
            s += p * c * c
            if s > M:
                return False
        return True

    lo = 0
    hi = 10**18  # ok(hi) is False
    while hi - lo > 1:
        mid = (lo + hi) // 2
        if ok(mid):
            lo = mid
        else:
            hi = mid

    L = lo
    units = 0
    cost = 0
    nxt = 10**30
    for p in P:
        c = (L + p) // (p << 1)
        units += c
        cost += p * c * c
        nm = p * ((c << 1) + 1)
        if nm < nxt:
            nxt = nm
    rem = M - cost
    ans = units + rem // nxt
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

import sys
from math import isqrt

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N = data[0]
    M = data[1]
    P = data[2:]
    max_p = max(P)

    def ok(x):
        total = 0
        for p in P:
            c = (x + p) // (p << 1)
            total += p * c * c
            if total > M:
                return False
        return True

    lo = 0
    hi = 2 * isqrt(M * max_p) + max_p + 2
    while hi - lo > 1:
        mid = (lo + hi) // 2
        if ok(mid):
            lo = mid
        else:
            hi = mid

    L = lo
    units = 0
    cost = 0
    nxt = 10**30
    for p in P:
        c = (L + p) // (p << 1)
        units += c
        cost += p * c * c
        nm = p * ((c << 1) + 1)
        if nm < nxt:
            nxt = nm

    rem = M - cost
    print(units + rem // nxt)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

cost = 0

    units += c
    cost += p * c * c

Let's maybe write final code with comments minimal:

import sys
from math import isqrt

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N = data[0]
    M = data[1]
    P = data[2:2+N]

    min_p = min(P)
    max_p = max(P)

    def ok(x, P=P, M=M, min_p=min_p):
        if x < min_p:
            return True
        s = 0
        for p in P:
            c = (x + p) // (p << 1)
            s += p * c * c
            if s > M:
                return False
        return True

    lo = 0
    hi = 2 * isqrt(M * max_p) + max_p + 2
    while hi - lo > 1:
        mid = (lo + hi) // 2
        if ok(mid):
            lo = mid
        else:
            hi = mid

    L = lo
    units = 0
    cost = 0
    for p in P:
        c = (L + p) // (p << 1)
        units += c
        cost += p * c * c

    ans = units + (M - cost) // (L + 1)
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "ok" if x=1, P=1,N=2e5,M=1: c=1, total after first1, after second2>M false. Good.


</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys
from math import isqrt

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N = data[0]
    M = data[1]
    P = data[2:2 + N]

    min_p = min(P)
    max_p = max(P)

    def ok(x, P=P, M=M, min_p=min_p):
        if x < min_p:
            return True
        total = 0
        for p in P:
            c = (x + p) // (p << 1)
            total += p * c * c
            if total > M:
                return False
        return True

    lo = 0
    hi = 2 * isqrt(M * max_p) + max_p + 2

    while hi - lo > 1:
        mid = (lo + hi) // 2
        if ok(mid):
            lo = mid
        else:
            hi = mid

    L = lo

    units = 0
    cost = 0
    for p in P:
        c = (L + p) // (p << 1)
        units += c
        cost += p * c * c

    ans = units + (M - cost) // (L + 1)
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
